Legend: **Action -> target** uses the ledger action vocabulary; a target in `code` is a page id in this file. `!` marks destructive elements, `~` marks host wiring of a control that is also listed on an `embedded-*` page (see README).

## Shell and navigation

### system

**[System entry points: share, launcher shortcuts, links, notifications]** · overlay · `_OcAppState` · `lib/main.dart:259`

Non-UI navigation handled by OcApp: each element is one external trigger and where it lands. Nothing is sent on the user's behalf; an undeliverable trigger is consumed with a snackbar notice and usually opens '/servers' (_showServersForLaunch, skipped when '/servers' is already on top).

### project-folder-browser

**Open a project** · sheet · `FolderBrowserSheet` · `lib/ui/widgets/folder_browser.dart:131`

A server on this phone: OpenCode inside the app (folders read from the built-in Ubuntu's files, so it works with the server stopped) or the OpenCode server this app runs in Termux (a short read-only script run inside Termux's Ubuntu through TermuxBridge.run, the path passed base64-encoded, bounded to 15 s in Termux and 25 s in the app). Browses from /root/projects up to /. A project (git repository, a project OpenCode knows, or any folder straight in /root/projects) opens with a tap; other folders are gone into. One primary opens the folder shown (never the home folder, / or the projects folder itself); New project makes a folder inside the folder shown (the app's Ubuntu: mkdir + git init; Termux: mkdir -p); Enter a path opens the path dialog. Remote servers, and Termux when it cannot run the app's commands, skip this sheet and go straight to the path dialog. Layout (2026-10-03, owner review): the sheet opens on a start page, title Open a project with Close in the header and no place line, menu or folder list: 1. New project (prominent; the name step swaps in place with "Creates <folder>/<name>", Create and open and a quiet Change folder that picks the parent in the browser and comes back; default folder is the host’s project space, else Projects on the phone’s storage), 2. Search this phone (phone-hosted servers only; the All files access consent first; a calm search step with a timer, a bar against the 10 s cap, folders checked and projects found, results as they come, Stop; then "<m> found in 0:09", Look deeper after the cap, an empty state with a way forward; back cancels), 3. Opened before (the recent folders with a Git badge when they hold .git, checked after and kept for the sheet; a tap opens; hidden when there are none). A quiet Choose a folder at the bottom opens the browser (one header row with back, the folder name and a more menu with Show hidden folders and Enter a path; the place menu when both places exist; one panel of folder rows; the pinned Open <folder>). A remote server keeps its own path sheet and has no search row because OpenCode lists files only inside a project.

Gates: `isInAppServer(controller.profile, linux)`; `or isManagedPhoneProfile(profile) and TermuxBridge.capabilities(): installed, service available, protocol supported, permission granted`

### phone-setup-progress

**Setting up OpenCode on this phone** · screen · `PhoneSetupProgressScreen` · `lib/ui/screens/phone_setup/phone_setup_progress_screen.dart:21`

Phone setup v2 screen B: a thin host for SetupProgressView. The job runs natively under a foreground service, so leaving this screen never stops it; coming back re-reads it. A first setup hands off to screen C (Ready) when done; an update or added tools just close back to where they were opened. The Termux host (PhoneSetupTermuxScreen, lib/ui/screens/phone_setup/phone_setup_termux_screen.dart, slice P1.3/P1.5 (2026-09-27)) draws the old Termux wizard as the same SetupProgressView checklist: Get Termux and Connect Termux once are rows only the person can do, then Linux base, OpenCode and Start OpenCode follow the Termux manager; a failure is a failed row with Continue setup. A first setup ends on home-shell, an update or a switch closes back to This phone. The persisted Termux job variant (PhoneSetupTermuxJobScreen, P5.3) also hosts SetupProgressView and hands first setup completion to Ready; its Get Termux, permission and resume actions are listed here.

Reached from: `embedded-local-agent-onboarding-block`, `phone-setup-start`, `servers-welcome`, `system`, `termux-setup-installed`, `termux-setup-switch-runtime-sheet`

### team-conversation

**<task title>** · screen · `TeamConversationScreen` · `lib/ui/screens/chat/team_conversation_view.dart:37`

The AI Team task as a conversation (docs/design/team-conversation-2026-09-26.md): the task's prompt, the lead's narrated reply (a step that is the task itself is 'it', never its words again), the agent strip under the header, tappable steps, gate cards, a 'No progress for …' notice with Nudge/Restart/Report when the task has stalled, the merge section, and a composer that messages the worker (or planner) through the team's own message control. Task details (menu) holds what the retired run page showed.

### team-agent

**<agent name> (Agent · session {id})** · screen · `AgentScreen` · `lib/ui/screens/team/agent_screen.dart:54`

Agent detail, a short status page (slice-P3.6): state from its session, what it works on, the newest step, capability-gated controls in the overflow and technical details. Its one pinned action, Open conversation, opens the worker's conversation in watching mode (chat), or the same watching page drawn from the team's live output (chat-watching-live) when the connected server cannot read the session; messaging the worker happens in that conversation's composer.

### settings

**Settings** · tab · `SettingsScreen` · `lib/ui/screens/settings_screen.dart:61`

The one Settings hub (root tab 4 of the shell, also pushed by Ctrl/Cmd+,): a search field, then five groups of at most five rows (target-ia §1.3, slice-P3.10): the server under its own name (This server, Saved servers, On this phone), Agent (Model, Providers and accounts, Tools, AI Team), Conversations (Always allowed actions, Show reasoning, Show timestamps and usage, Default shell, Voice), This app (Notifications, Keep running, Appearance, Privacy and data, Usage) and an unlabelled Help panel (Setup guide, Report a problem, Available on this server, About). Row titles, keywords, gates and tap actions come from the app-wide search index (lib/ui/search/search_index.dart); the hub decides order, the live values and the two switches. Rows the connected server cannot serve are absent and each group says how many in one muted line with a Why (KitGroupNote) that opens Available on this server. Typing also lists what sits inside second-level screens ('Inside settings') and places outside Settings ('Go to').

Reached from: `command-palette-dialog`, `global-shortcuts`, `home-shell`, `system`
